# Round 4 coverage matrix — Task 9 preparation draft

This is a non-executed, gitignored preparation artifact for Task 9 Step 1. It clones all 1,170 logical round-3 rows with
their IDs and criteria unchanged, with empty results, and appends the binding round-4 delta. It is not the canonical
round-4 matrix and cannot authorize coverage execution. Full coverage remains gated on Task 8 smoke being green.

**Candidate (Task 8): UNKNOWN.** CLI version/source SHA/executable SHA256, runner version/source SHA/bundle hashes,
demo SHA, WDA-cache manifest, and release-runner-home manifest all remain `UNKNOWN` until Task 8 records the exact
candidate. This draft does not claim a new binary or reuse round-3 validation.

**Draft logical counts:** 1,314 rows: CLI 431, iOS 361, Android 347, iOS+Android 175. Expanding shared rows yields
1,489 effective slice results: CLI 431, iOS 536, Android 522. The delta is 144 logical rows / 160 effective results.

**Descriptive Task 9 device mapping only (no device has been created or touched):** iOS `cov4-ios-26` (iOS 26.0),
`cov4-ios-27` (iOS 27.0), with row-scoped extras `cov4-ios-3`/`cov4-ios-4`; Android `cov4-android`, with row-scoped
extra `cov4-android-2`. Read historical `cov-ios-1`/`cov2-ios-1`/`cov3-ios-26` as `cov4-ios-26`, historical
`cov-ios-2`/`cov2-ios-2`/`cov3-ios-27` as `cov4-ios-27`, historical `cov*-ios-3`/`-4` as the corresponding
`cov4-ios-3`/`-4`, and historical `cov-android`/`cov2-android`/`cov3-android` (plus their `-2` forms) as the
corresponding `cov4-android` device. Historical fake-device names remain deliberately unmapped.

**Runner/version literal interpretation:** the Task 9 candidate runner version, stamp, source SHA and bundle hashes are
`UNKNOWN` until the final Task 8 manifest is recorded. In inherited rows, historical literals such as
`1.1.28-grantiva.11`, `1.1.28-grantiva.13` and `1.1.28-grantiva.13+android-drivers-2` mean the exact bundled runner
version and stamp in that final Task 8 manifest wherever the row says the literal follows the bundled runner. Historical
versions remain literal only when a row explicitly inspects historical source or migration text; they do not bind the
round-4 candidate.

**App and flow mapping:** the landmarks-demo candidate SHA is `UNKNOWN` until the final Task 8 manifest records it;
use its `ios/` and `android/` projects. The favorite flow's swipe action label is `Remove from favorites`, which
`.maestro/02-favorite.yaml` taps. The stock flows remain `.maestro/01`–`12` and `.maestro/99-crash.yaml`, with coverage
flows/configs under `.maestro/coverage/`. In inherited rows, `NEW:<name>` means
`.maestro/coverage/<name>.yaml`; `NEW:cfg/<name>` means `.maestro/coverage/cfg/<name>.grantiva.yml` on iOS and
`.maestro/coverage/cfg/<name>.grantiva-android.yml` on Android; `relaunch` means `cfg/relaunch.yaml`. The Android
`10-seed-many` flow asserts `Landmark 28`. Before execution, rewrite demo config device names to the descriptive
Task 9-owned `cov4-*` mapping above; never use their historical `qa-ios-1` or `Pixel_8_API_35` values.

**Expectation-correction provenance:** the only carried corrections are the approved round-3 corrections in
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-coverage-issues-round3/00-expectation-corrections-round3.md`, already embedded in
the inherited matrix and listed by logical row ID in `task-9-criteria-index.json`. They are carried forward into round 4;
this draft introduces no new expectation correction or relaxation.

**Protected devices:** never touch owner simulator `iPhone 17 Pro` (`B27D7D31-1E5E-47E1-8B9C-6C92D6B2AC4C`),
owner simulator `iPhone 18 Pro` (`FE441EE5-BBFC-4981-B68A-59C0DBCE26D6`), or AVD `Pixel_8_API_35`.
Every future device command must name its Task 9-owned device. Never use global teardown, kill by pattern, the default
runner home, auth, console, account credentials, network credentials, or real hardware. `GRANTIVA_API_KEY` remains unset.

## Verdicts and slices

`result` is left empty here. Slices write exactly one of: `pass`; `fail` (with evidence; a doubtful or inconsistent
behaviour is `fail`); `blocked <reason>` (environment or a Global Constraint, e.g. `teardown --all`); `n/a <reason>`
(the row does not apply on that platform).

- **platform:** `cli` (no device), `ios`, `android`, `both` (one verdict per platform; the slice records each half).
- **slice:** `CLI` rows need no device and run in Task 3. `iOS`/`Android` rows run in Tasks 4/5; `iOS+Android` rows are
  run by both device slices (each records its platform's half). Device rows run on iOS 26.0 (`cov3-ios-26`) unless the
  row names 27.0; Task 4 repeats every flow-command row and the stock set on 27.0 (`cov3-ios-27`).
- **flow:** as in round 1: `none` for device-free rows; a device row that drives no app names why in brackets; otherwise
  a stock path, a coverage flow or config, a brief Repro flow written to scratch, or the stock screens.
- Rules from round 1 that still hold: a usage error (`ValidationError`) exits 64; a `GrantivaError` exits 1; MCP rows are
  driven with the stdio send script or client from the round-1 evidence; a tool failure is a `result` with
  `"isError": true`, never a JSON-RPC `error` object (docs/mcp.md error paragraph now covers every tool).
- Round-1 and round-2 sections that refer to "the 2.0.1 matrix", "`(changed)`", "51c597a", "5eab55d" or "c65fc17"
  describe the row's history. The Task 9 binary, runner, stamp, bundle, demo and cache values come only from the final
  Task 8 manifest; until then they remain `UNKNOWN` and no historical round-3 value binds the round-4 candidate.
- Round 3: a row re-run after a fix round or on a quieter host keeps its original result line and adds a `<ID> rerun`
  line; a slice that believes an expected text is wrong records `fail (matrix/brief error — triage)` and never edits it.
- **Android L77 / inherited B3-L49 execution setup:** before the literal `launchApp: {clearState: true}` → bare
  `stopApp` → `assertNotVisible: "Landmarks"` flow, foreground neutral Settings on the row-owned AVD. Keep the quoted
  flow and assertion literal and unchanged. After stop, capture exact target-package `pidof`/process absence and the
  returned Settings-focused hierarchy. This isolates the independently proven NexusLauncher predicted-app label
  collision without weakening or regrading the assertion. (controller ledger Ruling in `progress.md`; accepted
  `task-2-live-review.md` Neutral-Settings qualification and preserved-failure record)


## CLI slice (device-free)

Work in scratch dirs (`$S=/private/tmp/.../scratchpad/cov-cli/<row>`), copies of the landmarks-demo projects where a
project is needed (`cp -c -R`), never in the landmarks-demo checkout. Exit codes: a usage error (`ValidationError`) is
64; a `GrantivaError` (`Error: Invalid argument: ...` and similar) is 1.

## Android slice

AVD `cov-android` (API 35, serial recorded at boot); `GRANTIVA_SESSION_ID=cov-android`; the scratch copy of
landmarks-demo/android has `emulator: cov-android` in `grantiva-android.yml` (the stock file names `Pixel_8_API_35`,
which no row may touch); variant `freeDebug`, application ID `com.kylebrowning.landmarks`. `cov-android-2` exists only
inside a row. Shell preamble per the plan's Global Constraints.

## Flow-command rows

Every Maestro-format command and selector form README:143-146 and README:172 list, once per platform (iOS rows are
repeated on 27.0 in Task 4 Step 2). Hooks H1/H2 are specified in the demo-flows doc.

| IOS-203 | `run --flow` tapOn {id:} | `tapOn: {id: "clock"}`, `assertVisible: {id: "clock"}` on Caching Demo | Matches the accessibility identifier (iOS) / resource id (Android), not text (README:172; docs/mcp.md:88; brief C08) | ios | iOS | NEW:01-selectors |  |
| IOS-204 | `run --flow` selector regex | `tapOn: "Golden Gate.*"`; `assertVisible: ".*Lake.*"` | Case-insensitive regex over the whole text: matches; `assertVisible: "Lake"` alone does not match `Lake Tahoe` (README:143-145) | ios | iOS | NEW:01-selectors |  |

Runner flows follow the runner rule (docs/mcp.md:88: `text` is a case-insensitive regex over the whole value, `exact: true` is the whole value as written); screens steps follow README:143-147 (docs/mcp.md:88; README:143-147; obs exact on Android) | ios | iOS | NEW:01-selectors |  |

## MCP rows

Every tool in docs/mcp.md (22) against a live session per platform, without a session, and without a config file;
plus the error shape, N12 and N13 (N rows). The 2.0.1 MCP rows keep their IOS/AND IDs. Live-session rows start the
session from the scratch project directory (`runner start --detach`, or `run --keep-alive` with the named flow) and
the server with `--project-dir` set to that same directory spelling.

## Brief rows (B)

One row per acceptance criterion of the 67 original briefs and R01-R05, the full criterion quoted verbatim as the
expected text (multi-line bullets joined; an ellipsis inside a quote is the brief's own). Criteria that ask for unit tests are checked in the CLI
slice by locating the test in `Tests/` at 51c597a (no `swift build`); criteria that ask for a CHANGELOG entry are
checked against README/docs/release notes, since CHANGELOG.md was removed in #179. A criterion whose fix chose the
other allowed alternative passes when the chosen alternative holds.

## Round 2 brief rows (B2)

One row per round-2 brief file in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-coverage-issues/` (K01-K110 with the -a/-b
splits, plus K111), its Acceptance criteria quoted as the expected text (multi-line bullets joined, bullets separated
by ` • `). The slice follows the brief's Platforms line: Android-only and iOS-only briefs stay on their platform;
`both` briefs, and briefs seen on one platform whose text says the code path is shared and untested on the other, are
`iOS+Android`; `n/a` briefs and briefs whose repro needs no device are `CLI`. Two briefs are split where their criteria
need different slices: K37 (`.1` device-free, `.2` MCP build on iOS) and K100 (`.1` no session, `.2` session starting).
Criteria that ask for a unit or runner test, a help string or a doc line are checked by the row's own slice by locating
them in the CLI source at 5eab55d (`Sources/`, `Tests/`, `README.md`, `docs/`) or the runner source at
1.1.28-grantiva.10; no `swift build` or `swift test` in a slice. A criterion offering alternatives passes when the
alternative the fix wave chose holds (the fix-wave rulings table records the choice for K09, K21-a, K23-b, K32, K34-b,
K48, K51, K53, K58, K60, K74, K78, K81, K86-b, K91, K94, K101, K106, K10). K09 and K94 carry the corrected expectation
(00-expectation-corrections.md). K111 is the one open brief: its row is expected to be `fail` unless fixed.

coverage-campaign e27f0a4): L01-L03 Android, L04 iOS. They are open; grade them on the current binary.

| B2-L01 | brief L01: Android inputText fails on the fixed 10 s HTTP timeout of the key-actions request even though the text was typed | 3 x `run --no-build --flow .maestro/03-edit.yaml --report-dir r$i` alone on `cov2-android` (`emulator ensure` defaults, software rendering; record `hw.gpu.*` from its config.ini), then the stock set; the `inputText " Renamed"` step duration | On a software-rendered API 35 AVD (`emulator ensure` defaults, `hw.gpu.mode` resolving to lavapipe), 03-edit passes 3 of 3 runs alone and inside the stock set (12/12 with 10-seed-many per L03), and `inputText " Renamed"` finishes within the step timeout. • A request that is still in progress when a fixed transport deadline would have expired is governed by the step's deadline instead (context from the step, or a per-request timeout at least the remaining step time); no UIAutomator2 call is cut at a hard-coded 10 s while its step still has time. • If the key-actions request does time out, the runner reads the focused field; when it holds the expected `before + text`, the step passes (optionally with a note that the server answered late); otherwise it fails naming UIAutomator2 and the timeout, without repeating the cause. • Typing does not grow linearly into the timeout: either chunk long text into several `/actions` requests or size the deadline from the character count; an 8-character append on the slow AVD takes well under the step timeout. • Runner tests: pkg/uiautomator2 — a fake server that answers `/actions` after 12 s with a 30 s step context succeeds, and one that never answers fails at the step deadline, not at 10 s. pkg/driver/uiautomator2 (next to input_append_test.go) — `SendKeyActions` returns a timeout error but the fake field then reads "Golden Gate Bridge Renamed": `inputText` succeeds; the field still reads "Golden Gate Bridge": it fails. Compare direct in-flow UIAutomator2 row movement and terminal CLI semantic artifacts across renderers; do not require a competing CLI hierarchy/dump during the owned pre-hold window. Preserve pointer coordinates and exact driver/source tests. (brief L03; corrected round 4) | android | Android | .maestro/10-seed-many.yaml |  |
| B2-L04 | brief L04: On iOS a `simctl diagnose` started while the runner shuts down outlives `runner stop` and keeps holding the simulator | 3 cycles on `cov2-ios-3` (`--device-type "iPhone 17" --runtime 26.0`): `runner start --detach --json`, `runner stop --json`, `ps -axo command= \| grep -i <UDID>` at once and 60 s later (read only); then `simulator teardown --udid <UDID>` without `--force` | After `runner stop` returns on iOS, `ps -axo command= \| grep -i <UDID>` shows no grantiva-runner, xcodebuild or `simctl diagnose` process, immediately and 60 s later, in 3 of 3 start/stop cycles. `runner stop` does this by reaping, after the group stop, every process whose command line names the session's UDID and matches the reaper's runner/WebDriverAgent/diagnose kinds (one late pass like forceTeardown's), or by preventing the diagnose being started. • The stop result reports what it reaped beyond the runner (e.g. `"reaped": [{"kind": "diagnose", "pid": ...}]` in `--json`, one line in text), so a leftover is visible rather than silent. • `grantiva simulator teardown --udid <UDID>` / `--session-id` without `--force` is not blocked by a leftover diagnose: it reaps diagnose processes for the device it is deleting (or `runner stop` guarantees none exist), and deletes the simulator. • The runner's WebDriverAgent `Stop()` ends xcodebuild in a way that does not leave a diagnostics process behind, or waits for/kills what it started for that UDID before returning. • Tests: GrantivaCLITests — `RunnerStopCommand.perform` with a fake process snapshot that still lists `xcrun simctl diagnose ... <UDID>` after `terminateGroup` returns `.interrupted` reaps that pid and reports it; GrantivaCoreTests — `SimulatorReaper.processes(owning:)` matches the exact diagnose command line captured by the repro. Runner (pkg/driver/wda, runner_lifecycle_test.go): `Stop()` with a fake command that spawns a child leaves no child alive. ## Round 3 brief rows (B3)

One or more rows per round-3 brief (L05-L62 with the -a/-b splits, and K111): the expected text quotes the measurable
part of the brief's Acceptance criteria (timings, exact messages, exit codes, JSON keys), bullets separated by ` • `,
with `(brief L<nn>)` as the source. Where a fix-wave ruling changed the acceptance (L10, L15, L24, L36, L38-a, L38-b,
L48), the row states the ruling and says so. Criteria that ask for a unit or runner test, a help string or a doc line are
checked by locating them in the CLI source at the round-3 main (`Sources/`, `Tests/`, `README.md`, `docs/`) or the runner
source at grantiva/main after runner #23 (fill: <SHA>) (marked "source check"); no `swift build`, `swift test` or `go test` in a slice. A
criterion offering alternatives passes when the shipped behaviour matches one of them. L51, L53, L61 and L62 are open for round 4:
their rows grade the documented current behaviour (L52 was fixed in CLI #205; L54-L60 are fixed in CLI #205 / runner #23 and use the rulings). Rows marked "default-device guard" follow the plan's rule; rows
needing load follow the plan's load rule.
